Um… I guess the thing that finally made me come in is I’m not really eating like I used to. Like, it’s gotten to where I’ll be at work, on my lunch break at the store, and I’ll buy something I normally like—stuff I used to actually look forward to—and then I just kind of sit there staring at it. I’ll take maybe a couple bites, or sometimes not even that, and then I end up throwing most of it away.

It feels stupid saying that out loud, because it’s just lunch, you know, but it’s been bothering me. I work retail, so lunch break is kind of one of the only times in the day where I’m supposed to just sit down and eat, and lately I just… don’t want it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, I think that’s actually been happening a lot. It’s not even like I’m always sitting there crying or something, it’s more like everything that used to make me feel like myself just feels kind of… flat now.

Like, I used to get really excited about gallery nights around town. I’d hear about one and plan around it, even if I was tired from work, because I liked seeing what people made and just being around that whole thing. And basketball too—I used to like playing, even just casually, not like I was amazing or anything, but it felt good to run around and joke with people. Lately if someone mentions going, I’ll say I’m busy or I’m too tired, but honestly I just don’t feel that pull to go.

Same with little stuff, like getting food-truck tacos with people. That used to be something I’d actually look forward to, standing around, talking, trying different places. Now I’ll go sometimes and just kind of stand there feeling like I’m not really in it, and then I’m looking for a reason to leave early.

And there’s this art stuff I used to help with for kids sometimes, and I liked that a lot before. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately even that feels hard to care about. I’ll show up and be there physically, but inside it’s like nothing really lands. I hate saying that because it makes me sound cold, and I don’t want to be that way. I just… don’t feel the same interest in things anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.

Like at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. Like I don’t deserve it. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.

People tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s kind of embarrassing because it doesn’t always match what I’m actually doing. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.

Even little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.

And I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”

I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.\n\nLike at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. Like I don’t deserve it. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.\n\nPeople tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual, even if you haven’t been doing much, or that you don’t have the same energy to get through the day as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.\n\nEven little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.\n\nAnd I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.

Especially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.

Sometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.

That used to be something I’d actually look forward to, standing around, talking, trying different places. Now I’ll go sometimes and just kind of stand there feeling like I’m not really in it, and then I’m looking for a reason to leave early.\n\nAnd there’s this art stuff I used to help with for kids sometimes, and I liked that a lot before. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately even that feels hard to care about. I’ll show up and be there physically, but inside it’s like nothing really lands. I hate saying that because it makes me sound cold, and I don’t want to be that way. Like I can get through the day and talk to customers and do what I’m supposed to do, and then when I’m by myself it’s not always like I’m actively sad, it’s just… I don’t feel like much is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice it more when I’m sitting at home and there’s nothing distracting me, but I don’t know if that counts as low mood or just being tired. Like I don’t deserve it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.

Like if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.

Same with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.

So yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.

I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.\n\nLike at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.\n\nPeople tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual, even if you haven’t been doing much, or that you don’t have the same energy to get through the day as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s kind of embarrassing because it doesn’t always match what I’m actually doing. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.\n\nEven little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.\n\nAnd I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts feel slower than usual, or that it's harder to focus or make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.\n\nLike if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.\n\nSame with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt like life isn’t worth living, or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had it in the way you’re asking, like where I’ve sat down and thought, “I don’t want to be alive,” or anything clear like that.

Sometimes I do get really worn down and I’ll think things like, “I can’t keep feeling like this,” but that feels more like being overwhelmed than actually wanting to die. I don’t know if that makes sense. It’s more like I want everything to stop for a while, or I want my head to quiet down, not that I’ve made some decision or plan or something.

So… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts about not wanting to be alive. Not really.

What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess the thing that finally made me come in is I’m not really eating like I used to. Like, it’s gotten to where I’ll be at work, on my lunch break at the store, and I’ll buy something I normally like—stuff I used to actually look forward to—and then I just kind of sit there staring at it. I’ll take maybe a couple bites, or sometimes not even that, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nIt feels stupid saying that out loud, because it’s just lunch, you know, but it’s been bothering me. I work retail, so lunch break is kind of one of the only times in the day where I’m supposed to just sit down and eat, and lately I just… don’t want it. The food doesn’t seem appealing, even when I know I should be hungry. That’s what made me think maybe I should ask for help, because it doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s actually been happening a lot. It’s not even like I’m always sitting there crying or something, it’s more like everything that used to make me feel like myself just feels kind of… flat now.\n\nLike, I used to get really excited about gallery nights around town. I’d hear about one and plan around it, even if I was tired from work, because I liked seeing what people made and just being around that whole thing. And basketball too—I used to like playing, even just casually, not like I was amazing or anything, but it felt good to run around and joke with people. Lately if someone mentions going, I’ll say I’m busy or I’m too tired, but honestly I just don’t feel that pull to go.\n\nSame with little stuff, like getting food-truck tacos with people. That used to be something I’d actually look forward to, standing around, talking, trying different places. Now I’ll go sometimes and just kind of stand there feeling like I’m not really in it, and then I’m looking for a reason to leave early.\n\nAnd there’s this art stuff I used to help with for kids sometimes, and I liked that a lot before. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately even that feels hard to care about. I’ll show up and be there physically, but inside it’s like nothing really lands. I hate saying that because it makes me sound cold, and I don’t want to be that way. I just… don’t feel the same interest in things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or having a low mood most of the time, even outside of work or when you’re alone?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it “down” all the time exactly. It’s more like I feel kind of muted, if that makes sense? Like I can get through the day and talk to customers and do what I’m supposed to do, and then when I’m by myself it’s not always like I’m actively sad, it’s just… I don’t feel like much is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice it more when I’m sitting at home and there’s nothing distracting me, but I don’t know if that counts as low mood or just being tired. I guess I haven’t really been keeping track of it in that way. It’s not like I wake up every morning and think, “I’m depressed,” or anything. It’s more that things feel heavier than they used to, but I’m not sure how much of the time that’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.\n\nLike at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. Like I don’t deserve it. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.\n\nPeople tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual, even if you haven’t been doing much, or that you don’t have the same energy to get through the day as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s kind of embarrassing because it doesn’t always match what I’m actually doing. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.\n\nEven little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.\n\nAnd I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts feel slower than usual, or that it's harder to focus or make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.\n\nLike if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.\n\nSame with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living, or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had it in the way you’re asking, like where I’ve sat down and thought, “I don’t want to be alive,” or anything clear like that.\n\nSometimes I do get really worn down and I’ll think things like, “I can’t keep feeling like this,” but that feels more like being overwhelmed than actually wanting to die. I don’t know if that makes sense. It’s more like I want everything to stop for a while, or I want my head to quiet down, not that I’ve made some decision or plan or something.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts about not wanting to be alive. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts feel slower than usual, or that it's harder to focus or make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.\n\nLike if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.\n\nSame with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living, or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had it in the way you’re asking, like where I’ve sat down and thought, “I don’t want to be alive,” or anything clear like that.\n\nSometimes I do get really worn down and I’ll think things like, “I can’t keep feeling like this,” but that feels more like being overwhelmed than actually wanting to die. I don’t know if that makes sense. It’s more like I want everything to stop for a while, or I want my head to quiet down, not that I’ve made some decision or plan or something.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts about not wanting to be alive. Not really.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say that you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just occasionally or in response to specific things?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "patient_turn_index: 9\n(If 1, this is your first reply — return your chief complaint.)\nReturn the JSON now."
  }
]

{"key":"None","name":"None","description":"None","reason":"The doctor is asking about persistently depressed mood most of the day nearly every day, but the provided symptom profile does not include a distinct Depressed_Mood item."}

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m happy or anything, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing, and I don’t know if I can say that for sure.

There are definitely parts of the day where I feel really low or heavy, but sometimes it feels more tied to whatever I’m thinking about at that moment, or what’s happening around me. Like if I’m busy, I might not notice it as much, and then when things get quiet it kind of comes back. So I don’t know if that counts as most of the day or not.

I guess I’ve felt off for a while, but I’m not sure I’d describe it as being down all day, every day. It’s more like it comes in waves, or it’s sitting in the background and gets louder sometimes. Does that make sense?

What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess the thing that finally made me come in is I’m not really eating like I used to. Like, it’s gotten to where I’ll be at work, on my lunch break at the store, and I’ll buy something I normally like—stuff I used to actually look forward to—and then I just kind of sit there staring at it. I’ll take maybe a couple bites, or sometimes not even that, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nIt feels stupid saying that out loud, because it’s just lunch, you know, but it’s been bothering me. I work retail, so lunch break is kind of one of the only times in the day where I’m supposed to just sit down and eat, and lately I just… don’t want it. The food doesn’t seem appealing, even when I know I should be hungry. That’s what made me think maybe I should ask for help, because it doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s actually been happening a lot. It’s not even like I’m always sitting there crying or something, it’s more like everything that used to make me feel like myself just feels kind of… flat now.\n\nLike, I used to get really excited about gallery nights around town. I’d hear about one and plan around it, even if I was tired from work, because I liked seeing what people made and just being around that whole thing. And basketball too—I used to like playing, even just casually, not like I was amazing or anything, but it felt good to run around and joke with people. Lately if someone mentions going, I’ll say I’m busy or I’m too tired, but honestly I just don’t feel that pull to go.\n\nSame with little stuff, like getting food-truck tacos with people. That used to be something I’d actually look forward to, standing around, talking, trying different places. Now I’ll go sometimes and just kind of stand there feeling like I’m not really in it, and then I’m looking for a reason to leave early.\n\nAnd there’s this art stuff I used to help with for kids sometimes, and I liked that a lot before. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately even that feels hard to care about. I’ll show up and be there physically, but inside it’s like nothing really lands. I hate saying that because it makes me sound cold, and I don’t want to be that way. I just… don’t feel the same interest in things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or having a low mood most of the time, even outside of work or when you’re alone?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it “down” all the time exactly. It’s more like I feel kind of muted, if that makes sense? Like I can get through the day and talk to customers and do what I’m supposed to do, and then when I’m by myself it’s not always like I’m actively sad, it’s just… I don’t feel like much is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice it more when I’m sitting at home and there’s nothing distracting me, but I don’t know if that counts as low mood or just being tired. I guess I haven’t really been keeping track of it in that way. It’s not like I wake up every morning and think, “I’m depressed,” or anything. It’s more that things feel heavier than they used to, but I’m not sure how much of the time that’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.\n\nLike at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. Like I don’t deserve it. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.\n\nPeople tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual, even if you haven’t been doing much, or that you don’t have the same energy to get through the day as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s kind of embarrassing because it doesn’t always match what I’m actually doing. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.\n\nEven little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.\n\nAnd I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. I can’t get it to shut back off. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts feel slower than usual, or that it's harder to focus or make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.\n\nLike if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.\n\nSame with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living, or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had it in the way you’re asking, like where I’ve sat down and thought, “I don’t want to be alive,” or anything clear like that.\n\nSometimes I do get really worn down and I’ll think things like, “I can’t keep feeling like this,” but that feels more like being overwhelmed than actually wanting to die. I don’t know if that makes sense. It’s more like I want everything to stop for a while, or I want my head to quiet down, not that I’ve made some decision or plan or something.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts about not wanting to be alive. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just occasionally or in response to specific things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. Like if I’m busy, I might not notice it as much, and then when things get quiet it kind of comes back. So I don’t know if that counts as most of the day or not.\n\nI guess I’ve felt off for a while, but I’m not sure I’d describe it as being down all day, every day. I think… overall it’s been about two months. Maybe a little hard to pin down exactly, because at first I didn’t think of it like “something started,” you know? It just felt like I was tired or stressed or maybe I was overthinking things.

It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately even that feels hard to care about. I’ll show up and be there physically, but inside it’s like nothing really lands. I hate saying that because it makes me sound cold, and I don’t want to be that way. I just… don’t feel the same interest in things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or having a low mood most of the time, even outside of work or when you’re alone?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it “down” all the time exactly. It’s more like I feel kind of muted, if that makes sense? Like I can get through the day and talk to customers and do what I’m supposed to do, and then when I’m by myself it’s not always like I’m actively sad, it’s just… I don’t feel like much is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice it more when I’m sitting at home and there’s nothing distracting me, but I don’t know if that counts as low mood or just being tired. I guess I haven’t really been keeping track of it in that way. It’s not like I wake up every morning and think, “I’m depressed,” or anything. It’s more that things feel heavier than they used to, but I’m not sure how much of the time that’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.\n\nLike at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. Like I don’t deserve it. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.\n\nPeople tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual, even if you haven’t been doing much, or that you don’t have the same energy to get through the day as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s kind of embarrassing because it doesn’t always match what I’m actually doing. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.\n\nEven little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.\n\nAnd I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts feel slower than usual, or that it's harder to focus or make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.\n\nLike if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.\n\nSame with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living, or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had it in the way you’re asking, like where I’ve sat down and thought, “I don’t want to be alive,” or anything clear like that.\n\nSometimes I do get really worn down and I’ll think things like, “I can’t keep feeling like this,” but that feels more like being overwhelmed than actually wanting to die. I don’t know if that makes sense. It’s more like I want everything to stop for a while, or I want my head to quiet down, not that I’ve made some decision or plan or something.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts about not wanting to be alive. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just occasionally or in response to specific things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m happy or anything, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing, and I don’t know if I can say that for sure.\n\nThere are definitely parts of the day where I feel really low or heavy, but sometimes it feels more tied to whatever I’m thinking about at that moment, or what’s happening around me. Like if I’m busy, I might not notice it as much, and then when things get quiet it kind of comes back. So I don’t know if that counts as most of the day or not.\n\nI guess I’ve felt off for a while, but I’m not sure I’d describe it as being down all day, every day. It’s more like it comes in waves, or it’s sitting in the background and gets louder sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall — like things feel flat, you don’t have the same interest in activities, and everything feels heavier than before?\n\nPatient: I think… overall it’s been about two months. Maybe a little hard to pin down exactly, because at first I didn’t think of it like “something started,” you know? It just felt like I was tired or stressed or maybe I was overthinking things.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, it lines up pretty close with when I got that window display award at work, and then the district manager started paying attention to me. Everyone was making a big deal about it for a while, and after that is when things started feeling different. Like the stuff that used to feel normal or even kind of enjoyable started feeling heavier, flatter, like I had to force myself through it more.\n\nSo… yeah. I’d say around two months, since all that attention happened after the display award.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. I mean, I didn’t really want to call it that at first, because it sounds dramatic, like I suddenly hate everything, and it’s not exactly that. It’s more like I’ll go through the motions and I know, in my head, that I used to like it, but I’m not really feeling anything from it.

Like gallery nights, those used to be something I’d actually look forward to. I’d think about what I wanted to see, or who might be there, or I’d get ideas for my own stuff. Now if I go, I kind of just stand around looking at the walls, and everyone else seems into it, and I’m just… flat. Like I’m there but not really there. Same with basketball. I used to like playing, even just messing around, getting some energy out. Lately I’ll show up and after a few minutes I’m already thinking of some excuse to leave.

Even little stuff, like grabbing tacos from the food trucks, that used to feel like a treat, you know? Or helping kids with art things, that used to make me feel useful. Now I’ll do it and I’m just waiting for it to be over. Then I feel bad because those are things I’m supposed to enjoy, or I used to enjoy them, and I can’t really explain why they don’t hit the same.

So, yeah… I guess I don’t get much pleasure or satisfaction from those things lately. I usually just make some excuse and leave early if I can.

I’ll take maybe a couple bites, or sometimes not even that, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nIt feels stupid saying that out loud, because it’s just lunch, you know, but it’s been bothering me. I work retail, so lunch break is kind of one of the only times in the day where I’m supposed to just sit down and eat, and lately I just… don’t want it. The food doesn’t seem appealing, even when I know I should be hungry. That’s what made me think maybe I should ask for help, because it doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think that’s actually been happening a lot. It’s not even like I’m always sitting there crying or something, it’s more like everything that used to make me feel like myself just feels kind of… flat now.\n\nLike, I used to get really excited about gallery nights around town. I’d hear about one and plan around it, even if I was tired from work, because I liked seeing what people made and just being around that whole thing. And basketball too—I used to like playing, even just casually, not like I was amazing or anything, but it felt good to run around and joke with people. Lately if someone mentions going, I’ll say I’m busy or I’m too tired, but honestly I just don’t feel that pull to go.\n\nSame with little stuff, like getting food-truck tacos with people. That used to be something I’d actually look forward to, standing around, talking, trying different places. Now I’ll go sometimes and just kind of stand there feeling like I’m not really in it, and then I’m looking for a reason to leave early.\n\nAnd there’s this art stuff I used to help with for kids sometimes, and I liked that a lot before. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately even that feels hard to care about. I’ll show up and be there physically, but inside it’s like nothing really lands. I hate saying that because it makes me sound cold, and I don’t want to be that way. I just… don’t feel the same interest in things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or having a low mood most of the time, even outside of work or when you’re alone?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it “down” all the time exactly. It’s more like I feel kind of muted, if that makes sense? Like I can get through the day and talk to customers and do what I’m supposed to do, and then when I’m by myself it’s not always like I’m actively sad, it’s just… I don’t feel like much is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice it more when I’m sitting at home and there’s nothing distracting me, but I don’t know if that counts as low mood or just being tired. I guess I haven’t really been keeping track of it in that way. It’s not like I wake up every morning and think, “I’m depressed,” or anything. It’s more that things feel heavier than they used to, but I’m not sure how much of the time that’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve described it that way before, but lately I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’m not really good enough for things people are saying I’m good enough for.\n\nLike at work, there was this award thing, and people acted like I earned it, and I know on paper I guess I did the job or whatever, but I keep thinking… no, they must not be seeing it right. Like I don’t deserve it. And then there’s been talk about maybe giving me a chance at supervisor, and instead of feeling proud or excited, I mostly feel this guilt in my stomach, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I’m better than I am.\n\nPeople tell me, “No, you didn’t trick anybody, you work hard,” and I try to hear that, but it doesn’t really stick. I just keep replaying it and thinking they’re going to realize they made a mistake. And then I feel bad for even being considered, like someone else should have gotten it and I’m taking up space I didn’t earn. It’s embarrassing, because I know it probably sounds like I’m fishing for reassurance, but it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I actually did something wrong, even though I can’t point to what.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual, even if you haven’t been doing much, or that you don’t have the same energy to get through the day as you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s kind of embarrassing because it doesn’t always match what I’m actually doing. Like I’ll be at work, and the first part of the shift I can usually make myself move around and act normal enough, but by the middle of it my body starts feeling really heavy.\n\nEven little things start feeling like they take way more out of me than they should. Straightening the racks, walking back to the stockroom, going to grab something for a customer… stuff I’ve done a million times without even thinking about it. Lately it’s like my legs don’t want to cooperate, or like I’m carrying extra weight around. Not pain exactly, just this drained feeling.\n\nAnd I’ll catch myself standing there for a second before I go do something, not because I’m trying to be lazy, but because it feels like I have to talk myself into moving. I don’t like that. I used to be able to get through a shift and still feel like I had something left in me, but now halfway through I’m already thinking, “How am I going to get through the rest of this?”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been kind of messed up, I guess. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling or checking the time and thinking, “If I fall asleep right now I can still get a little more,” but then that makes me more awake somehow. So yeah, it’s definitely not normal for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts feel slower than usual, or that it's harder to focus or make decisions, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening, actually. I didn’t really connect it to anything at first, because it sounds so small, but at work I keep losing my place on things that should be easy for me.\n\nLike if I’m counting inventory, I’ll get halfway through a shelf or a box and then suddenly I’m not sure what number I was on, so I have to start over. And then I start getting annoyed with myself because it’s not complicated math or anything, it’s just counting, but my brain feels like it slips off the track. I’ll be standing there staring at the items, trying to remember if I counted that row already, and then I second-guess it so much that I don’t trust whatever number I came up with.\n\nSame with setting up displays. Picking colors or deciding what should go where used to be something I could do pretty fast. I mean, maybe not perfect, but I could just make a choice and move on. Now I’ll stand there with two shirts or whatever and go back and forth like it’s some huge decision. I’ll think, “This one looks better,” and then immediately think, “No, that’s wrong, why would you pick that?” And then I waste time on it and feel stupid because it’s such a basic part of my job.\n\nSo yeah, I don’t know if my thoughts are slower exactly, but it feels harder to stay focused and harder to trust little decisions I used to make without thinking so much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living, or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had it in the way you’re asking, like where I’ve sat down and thought, “I don’t want to be alive,” or anything clear like that.\n\nSometimes I do get really worn down and I’ll think things like, “I can’t keep feeling like this,” but that feels more like being overwhelmed than actually wanting to die. I don’t know if that makes sense. It’s more like I want everything to stop for a while, or I want my head to quiet down, not that I’ve made some decision or plan or something.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts about not wanting to be alive. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just occasionally or in response to specific things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m happy or anything, but “depressed most of the day nearly every day” sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing, and I don’t know if I can say that for sure.\n\nThere are definitely parts of the day where I feel really low or heavy, but sometimes it feels more tied to whatever I’m thinking about at that moment, or what’s happening around me. Like if I’m busy, I might not notice it as much, and then when things get quiet it kind of comes back. So I don’t know if that counts as most of the day or not.\n\nI guess I’ve felt off for a while, but I’m not sure I’d describe it as being down all day, every day. It’s more like it comes in waves, or it’s sitting in the background and gets louder sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall — like things feel flat, you don’t have the same interest in activities, and everything feels heavier than before?\n\nPatient: I think… overall it’s been about two months. Maybe a little hard to pin down exactly, because at first I didn’t think of it like “something started,” you know? It just felt like I was tired or stressed or maybe I was overthinking things.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, it lines up pretty close with when I got that window display award at work, and then the district manager started paying attention to me. Everyone was making a big deal about it for a while, and after that is when things started feeling different. Like the stuff that used to feel normal or even kind of enjoyable started feeling heavier, flatter, like I had to force myself through it more.\n\nSo… yeah. I’d say around two months, since all that attention happened after the display award.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer get any pleasure or satisfaction out of things you used to enjoy, like the art events or playing basketball?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. Yeah… I think so. I didn’t really notice it at first because I kept telling myself, like, everybody gets tired at work, you know? Retail is standing around, moving stuff, dealing with people, going back and forth, so I thought maybe it was just normal.

But it feels different lately. By the middle of a shift my body starts feeling really heavy, like I’m wearing wet clothes or something. Even little things that shouldn’t be a big deal — straightening racks, folding things again, walking to the stockroom to grab something — it takes more out of me than it should. I’ll catch myself sort of standing there for a second before doing it, like I have to talk myself into moving.

And it’s not always after I’ve done some huge amount of work either. Sometimes the shift hasn’t even been that busy, and I still feel drained like I’ve already worked the whole day. I don’t know if I’d say I’m collapsing or anything, but yeah, it’s there a lot. Most days at work, especially halfway through, I feel worn down in a way that doesn’t really match what I’ve actually been doing.

I don’t like that. Not like I’m up all night every single night, but most nights I get in bed and then my brain just starts going. It’s like the second there’s nothing else happening, I start replaying stuff from the store.\n\nEspecially the award thing. I’ll go back over what people said, how they looked when they said it, whether they really meant it, whether they were just being nice, whether I looked stupid accepting it… and then I start thinking about the supervisor conversations too, like if they’re talking about me when I’m not there, or if they’re going to realize they picked the wrong person. It just keeps looping, and I’ll tell myself, “Just stop thinking about it,” but that doesn’t really do anything.\n\nSometimes I do eventually fall asleep, but then I’ll wake up way too early, like before I need to be up, and once I’m awake my mind turns right back on. I can’t get it to shut back off. By the middle of a shift my body starts feeling really heavy, like I’m wearing wet clothes or something. Even little things that shouldn’t be a big deal — straightening racks, folding things again, walking to the stockroom to grab something — it takes more out of me than it should. I’ll catch myself sort of standing there for a second before doing it, like I have to talk myself into moving.\n\nAnd it’s not always after I’ve done some huge amount of work either. Sometimes the shift hasn’t even been that busy, and I still feel drained like I’ve already worked the whole day. I don’t know if I’d say I’m collapsing or anything, but yeah, it’s there a lot. Most days at work, especially halfway through, I feel worn down in a way that doesn’t really match what I’ve actually been doing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like a significant decrease or increase in how much you eat or how much you weigh?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"What made me ask for help is I barely want to eat anymore, like I will be on my lunch break at the store staring at food I used to like and I just throw most of it away.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s actually one of the main things that made me think I should maybe ask for help, because I barely want to eat anymore.

Like, I’ll be on my lunch break at the store, and I’ll get something I used to like — something easy, nothing weird — and I’ll just sit there staring at it. I’ll take a few bites maybe, but it feels like I’m eating because I know I’m supposed to, not because I want it. And then most of it ends up in the trash. I’ll look at it like, “Why did I even buy this?” but then if I don’t buy anything, I also feel like I’m being stupid because I know I should eat during a shift.

It’s not like I’m trying to diet or anything. Food just doesn’t really seem appealing. Stuff I used to look forward to on break kind of feels like cardboard now, or like too much effort. I don’t know exactly how much weight I’ve lost, if I have, because I don’t really check all the time, but the appetite part, yeah… that’s definitely changed.
